Observer?</w:t></w:r></w:p><w:p><w:r><w:t xml:space="preserve">When a vehicle is dispatched to a delivery, all available couriers should be notified so they can decide to accept the job. Observer fully decouples the vehicle (subject) from the couriers (observers): the vehicle does not know which couriers exist, and couriers can be added or removed at runtime without touching the vehicle code.</w:t></w:r></w:p><w:p/><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="Heading3"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:b/><w:bCs/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Classes involved</w:t></w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle Availability</w:t></w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="Heading3"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:b/><w:bCs/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Why Proxy?</w:t></w:r></w:p><w:p><w:r><w:t xml:space="preserve">Fetching vehicle availability from the database is expensive. The Virtual Proxy sits in front of the real service and caches results keyed by country + weight. On repeated queries with the same parameters, the proxy returns the cached result instantly without hitting the database. The client (ShippingService) only knows the VehicleService interface and is unaware a proxy is involved.</w:t></w:r></w:p><w:p/><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="Heading3"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:b/><w:bCs/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Classes involved</w:t></w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="ListParagraph"/><w:numPr><w:ilvl w:val="0"/><w:numId w:val="2"/></w:numPr></w:pPr><w:r><w:t xml:space="preserve">RealVehicleService â€” real, expensive implementation</w:t></w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle
